MINISTRY OF PERSONNEL, PUBLIC GRIEVANCES & 
PENSIONS 

(Department of Personnel & Training) 

New Delhi, the 18th September, 1990 

S.O. 2582.—In exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to article 309 read with clause (5) of article 148 of 
the Constituion, and after consultation with the Comptroller 
and Auditor General of India in relation to persons serving 
in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, the President 
hereby makes the following ruels further to amend the Cen¬ 
tral Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964, namely:—■ 

L O) These rules may be called the Central Civil Services 
(Conduct) Amendment Rules, 1990. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their 
publication in the Official Gazette. 

2. In the Cential Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964, for 
Rule 13 of the following Rule shall be substituted, namely;— 

“13, GIFTS (1) Save as provided in these Rules, no Gov¬ 
ernment servant shall accept, or permit any member of his 
family or (any other person acting on his behalf) to accept, 
any gift, 

EXPLANATION,—The expression “gift” shall include free 
transport, hoarding lodging or other service or any other 
pecuniary advantage provided by any person other than a 
near relative or personal friend having no official dealings 
with the Govt, servant. 

NOTE (1)—A casual meal, lift or other social hospitality 
shall not be deemed to be a gift. 

NOTE (2) —A Government servant shall avoid accepting 
lavish hospitality or frequent hospitality from any individual, 
industrial or commercial firms, organisations, etc. having offi¬ 
cial dealing with him. 

(2) On occasions, such as wedding anniversaries, fune¬ 
rals or religious functions, when the making of a gift is in 
conformity with the prevailing religious or social practice, a 
Govt, servant may accept gitts from his near relatives but 
shall make report to the Government if the value of any gift 
exceeds:— 

(i) R Si 1000 in the case of a Government servant holding 
any Group A or Group B post; 

(Ji) Rs. 500 in the case of a Government servant holding 
any Group C post; and 

(iii) Rs. 200 in the case of a Government servant holding 
any Group D post. 

(3) On such occasions as are specified in sub-rule (2), 
a Government servant may accept gifts from his personal 







4357 


(5FT II--OT 3 (ii)] 


Wff xm^ ; 6, 1990/ KTforc 14, 19 3 2 


friends having no official dealing with him, but he shall 
make a report to the Government if the value of any such 
gift exceeds:— 

<0 Ra. 400 in the case of a Government servant holding 
any Group A or Group B post; 

(ii) Rs. 200 in the case of a Government servant holding 

any Group C post; and 

(iii) Rs. 100 in the case of a Government servant holding 

any Group D post. 

(4; In any other case, a Government servant shall not 
accept any gift without sanction of the Government if the 
value thereof exceeds. 


(i) Rs. 150 in the case of a Government servant holding 

any Group A or Group B post; 

(ii) Rs. 50 in the case of a Government servant holding 

any Group C or Group D post. 


(5) Notwithstanding anything contained in sub-rules (2), 
(3) and (4), a Government servant, being a member of Indian 
delegation or otherwise, may receive ajnd retain gifts from 
foreign dignitaries if the market value of gifts received on 
one occasion doos not exceed Rs. 1000. In all other cases, 
the acceptance and retention of such gifts shall bo regulated 
by the instructions issued by the Government in this regard 
from time to time. 


(6) A Government servant shall not uaccept any gift from 
any foreign firm which is either contracting with the Govern¬ 
ment of India or is one with which the Government servant 
had, has or is likely to have official dealing. Acceptance of 
gifts by a Government servant from any other firm shall be 
subject to the provisions of sub-rule (4),”

S.O. 2587.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (J) of Section 7 of the Export (Quality Control and 
Inspection) Act, 1963 (22 of 1963), the Central Government 
hereby recognises for a period of three year from the date 
ol publication of this notification M/s. inspection Survey and 
Surveillance (India) Pvt., Ltd,, 26D/27, Park Lane, - Cal- 
cnlta-700016 as an agency for inspection of Minerals and 
Ores (Gioup-JT) as specified in Schedule annexed hereto prior 
to export subject to the condition that the said agency shall 
give adequate facilities to any Officer nominated by the Ex¬ 
port Inspection Council in this behalf to examine the method 
of inspection followed by the said agency in granting the 
certificate of inspection under sub-rule (4) of rule 4 of the 
Export of Minerals and Ores Group-II (inspection) Rules, 
1965.

1. Manganese Dioxide. 

2. Kyanite, 

3. Sillbnanite. 

4. Zinc Ores, including zinc concentrates. 

5. Magnesite, including dead-bunt and calcined magnesite 

6 . Barytes. 

7. Fed Oxide. 

8. Yellow Ochre. 

9. Steatite. 

10. Feldspar.

Surveillance (India) Pvt. Ltd., 26D/27, Park Lane Calcutta- 
700016, as an agency for inspection of the Inorganic Pig¬ 
ments specified m Schedule annexed hereto prior to export 
subject to the condition that the said agency shall give ade¬ 
quate facilities to any officer nominated by the Export Inspec- 


6 , i 99 0/-mft^r 14, 1912 

t.on Ocuncd In this fcehali rt> examine the method of inspec¬ 
tion followed oy the a,d agency in giantm^ the certificate of 
inspection under sub-iult (4) of rule 4 of the Export of 
Inorganic Pigments (Inspection) Rules, 1966. 

SCHEDULE 

1. Zinc Oxide.

for Rs. 42,82287 (Rupees Forty two lakhs eighty two thou¬ 
sand two hundred and eighty seven only) for impoH of Die 
Steel Blocks under Free Foreign Exchange. 

2. The firm has applied for issue of Duplicate copy ot 
import licence for Customs purposes on the ground that the 
original import licence has been misplaced. It has further 
been stated that the import licence was not registered with 
any Customs Authority and as such the value of Customs 
Purposes copy has not been utilised at all.

affidavit on stamped paper duly sworn in before a Notary 
Public Kerala State. 1 am accordigly satisfied that the ori¬ 
ginal Customs Purposes copy of import licence No P/D/ 
2277059 dated 14-6-1989 ha s been Ion or misplaced by the 
firm. In exercise of the powers conferred under sub-clause 
9(cc) of the Import (Control) Order, 1955 dated 7-12-1955 
as amended the suid original import licence No. P[D|2277059 
dated 14-6-89 issued to M/s. Steel and Industial Fogings 
Ttd. is hereby cancelled.

MINISTRY OF ENERGY 
(Department of Coni) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 13th September, 1990 

SO. 2592.—’Whereas on the publication of the notification 
of the Government of India in the Ministry of Energy (De¬ 
partment of Coal) No. S.O. 1869 dated the 10th July, 1989, 
in the Gazette of India, Part If, Section 3, sub-section (ii) 
dated the 12th August, 1989 issued under sub-section (1) Of 
section 9 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Deve¬ 
lopment) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter referred to 
as the said Act), the lands and rights in or over ruch lands 
described in the Schedule appended to the said notification 
(hereinafter referred to os the said lands) vested absolutely 
in the Central Government free from all encumbrances sub¬ 
section (1 ) of sectiop 10 of the said Act; 

And whereas the Central Government is satisfied that the 
South Eastern Coalfields Limited. Biluspur (Madhva Pra¬ 
desh) (he'einaflcr referred to as the Government Company) 
is willing to comply with such terms and conditions as the 
Central Government thinks fit to impose in this behalf; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers confcried by sub¬ 
section (1) of section 11 of the said Act, the Central Gov¬ 
ernment hereby directs that the said lands and rights so 
vested shall, with effect froth the 12th August, 1989, instead 
of continuing to so vest in the Central Government, vest in 
the Government Company, subject to the following terms and 
conditions, namely ;— 

(1) The Government Company shall reimburse the 
Central Government all payments made in respect 
of compensation, interest, damages and the like, as 
determined under the provisions of the said Act. 

(2) A Tribunal shall be constituted for the purpose of 
determining the amounts payable to the Central 
Government by the Government Company undet 
condition (1) and all expediture incurred in com 
nection with any such Tribunal and persons appoint¬ 
ed to assist the Tribunal shall be borne bv the Gov- 


4361 


eminent Company and, similarly all expenditures 
incurred in respect of all legal proceedings like ap¬ 
peals etc, for or in connection with rights, in or 
over the said lands, sovesting shall also T e borne 
by the Government Company; 

(3) The Government. Company shall indemnify tie Cen¬ 
tral Government or its officials against any other 
expenditure that may be necessary in connection 
with any proceedings by or against the Central 
Government or its officials regarding the rights in 
or over the said lands so vesting; 

(4) The Government Company shall have no power to 
transfer the said lauds or rights to any other per¬ 
son without the previous approval of the Central 
Government; and 

(5) Tlie Govern men 1 " Company shall abide by such 
directions or conditions as may be given or im¬ 
posed bv the Central Government for particular 
areas of I he «ak1 lands, as and when necessary

S.O. 2602.—-Whereas it appears to the Centrol Gov¬ 
ernment that it is necessary in the public interest that for 
the transport of Petroleum and Natural Gas through 
TAT1PAKA-KAK1NADA pipeline is to be laid by the Gas 
Authority of India Ltd. 

And whereas it appears that lor the purpose of laying 
this pipeline it is necessary to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-Section (i) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum & Mine¬ 
rals pipelines (Acquisition of Right of users in the land) 


Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares it s intention to acquire the Right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Gas Authority of India Ltd. K.G, Basin Project, 
12/76, Prakash Nagar, Rajahmundry-533103, Andhra Pra¬ 
desh. 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by legal Practitioner.

S.O. 2606.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interested that for the 
transport of petroleum from EPS to Ankleshwar CTF in 
Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil and Natural 
Gas Commission. 

And, whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the hind described in the schedule annexed heieto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (B of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine¬ 
rals Pipeline (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein ; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodttra- 
3900(‘9. 

And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal practitioner.

S.O. 2609.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary m the puolic interest that tor the transport 
of petroleum Irom GNGM to BPS m Gujarat State pipeline 
should he laid by the Oil and Natural Gas Commission. 

And, whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (lj of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine¬ 
rals' Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein: 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to. 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpqra Road, Vadodara- 
390009. 

And every person making Buch an objection shall also state 
specifically whether ho wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal Practitioner.

S.O. 2610,—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the transport 
of petroleum from Fadra EPS to AKCL in Gujarat state 
pipeline should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas Com¬ 
mission. 

And, whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum und Mine¬ 
rals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein : 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil md Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara- 
390009. 

And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal Practitioner.

S.O. 2613.—Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas S.O, 
No. 1350 dated 14-4-88 under sub-section (1) of Section 3 
of the Petrolcuni and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right 
of User in Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Gov¬ 
ernment dedaied its intention to acquire the right of user 
in lands specified in the schedule appended to that notification 
for the purpose of laying pipeline. 

And, whereas, the Competent Authority has under sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 

And, further, whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands in the schedule appended to tihs notification: 

Now, therefore, in exercise of tho power conferred by sub¬ 
section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act. the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notifica¬ 
tion hereby acquired for laying the pipeline;

S.O. 2614.—VVheieas it appears to the Central Government 
that it ^ necessary in the public interest that for the trans¬ 
port of petroleum from Padra-9 to EPS Padra in Gujarat 
State pipelme should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas 
Commission. j 

And, wheists, it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is» necessary to acquire that right of usei 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section ft) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine¬ 
rals P pelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act. 1962 (50 of 1962),j the Central Government hereby 
declines its intention to acquire the right of user therein : 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may,

Authority.. Oil arid Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division Makarpura Road, Vadodara- 
390009.

legal Practitioner.

S.O. 2616.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the trans¬ 
port of petroleum from GMD1 (IT) to EPS in Gujarat State 
pipeline should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas Com¬ 
mission. 

And, whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine¬ 
rals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein ; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara- 
390009, 

And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal Practitioner.

'S O. 2623.—Whereas it appears to the Central Gove^' 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest toi the tians- 
port of petroleum from GNFU to EPS in Gujarat State 
pipeline should be laid by the Oil and Natural Gas Com¬ 
mission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquired that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto:— 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government here^ 
by declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil and Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara- 
390009.

state specifically whether he wishes to be hear in person or 
by legal practitioner.

S.O. 2635.—Whereas it appears to the Centra] Gov~ 
ernment that it is necessary in the public interest that for 
the transport of petroleum from GNEF to GNJ3I Header 
in Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil & 
Natural Gas Commission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government 
hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of user 
therein; 

Provided that any person Interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object 
to the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Construction & 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road; Vadodara-390009. 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be hear in person 
or by legal Practitioner.

S O, 2643.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for tho trans¬ 
port of petroleum from CPF to T\ Point in Gujarat State 
pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural Gas Commission, 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user In the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto* 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (AcquisiUpn of Right of User in the land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notillcation, object to the 
laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Construction <& 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road; Vadodara-390009. 

And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal Practitioner.

S.O. 2655.--Whereas a public notice for revising the local 
atea of MeciT Telephone System was published as required 
by rule 434(FT)(2C) of the Indian Telegraph Rules, 1951 in 
the Newspaper ; in circulation at Meerut, investing objections 
and suggestions freni all persons likely to be affected thereby, 
within a period of 30 days from the date of publication oi 
the notice hi the Newspapers; 

And whereas the sa : d notice was made available to the 
public on 841-89 in the ‘Dainik Amar Ujalah Dainik 
Hamara Yug\ ‘Dainik Jagran’ Meerut; 

And whereas no objections and suggestions have been re¬ 
ceived from the public on the said notice; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the power conferred by 
rule 434(I1I)(2C) of the said Rules, the Director General 
Telecommunications hereby declares that with effect from 
16-10-1990 the revised local area of Meerut Telephone 
System shall be as under; 

Meerut Telephone System.—The local area of Meerut 
Telephone System shall cover an area falling under the juris¬ 
diction of Meerut Municipal Corporation as noticed vide 
Uf. P. Government Gazette Notification No. 2193!fI-NV-7- 
121[K-78 dt. 11-9-87 and Meerut Cantonment Board; Provid¬ 
ed tha the telephone subscribers located ouNide the 
Menut Municipal Corporation and Meerut Cantonment 
Board limits but who are served from Meerut Telephonf 


[Part II—Sec, 3 (ii)] 

System shall continue to pay local tariffs as long as they are 
located within 5 kms of any exchange of this system ami 
remain connected to it. However, when an exchange is 
opened to serve these areas, these are liable to he connected 
to that exchange system.

consequential benefits and conducting/initiating enquiry on

The case of the management is that the service condition 
of the employees of FUl India arc regulated by the TCI 
Staff Regulation 1971 framed Linder the FCI Act. The con¬

With regard to continuation of enquiries on departmental 
proceeding against the concerned workman the management 
has started 2 separate proceedings against the concerned

The points for decision in this case arc * 

1. Whether the action of the management of FCI Patna 

by placing the concerned workman Sri Vijuyeudra 
Kumar under continuous suspension with effect 
from 23-3-82 till date is justified. 

2. Whether the action of the management in denying 

full wages and other benefits to the concerned 
workman even after resumption of his duty vide 
order dated 12-8-82 Is legal and justified and 

3. Whether the action of the management ip conduct¬ 

ing enquiry on dropped charges against the con 
cerned workman is legal and justified ? 

The workmen examined only WW-l who is the concerned 
" orkman Vijayendra Kumar blmseff. Tho management 
CAamined no witness. The documents of the management 
marked Ext. M-l to M-8 and the documents of tlw work¬ 
men are marked Ext. W-l to W-21. 

Point No. 2 

It u the admitted case of the parties that while the con¬ 
cerned workman Vijayendra Kumar was working at Khagaul 
FSD of FCI, he was suspended vide order of suspension Ext. 
W-l dated 23-3-82, The management has also got the sajd 
suspension order dated 23-3-82 marked as Bxt. M-l. It will 
appear from Ext. W-l that the concerned workman was 
suspended in contemplation of disciplinary proceeding against 
him under Regulation 66(i) of FCl Staff Regulation, 1971. 
No specific allegation appears to have been made in Ext. 
W-l, Ext. M-l which is the photo copy of the suspension 
order dated 23-3-82 shows that the contemplated disciplinary 
proceeding against the concerned workman was regarding 
misappropriation of huge quantity of fertiliser and also short¬ 
age of empty gunny bags which reason of suspension is given 
at the foot of Ext, M-l. It is submitted on behalf of the 
concerned workman that in his copy of th e suspension Ext- 
W-l the reason for suspension which is stated in Ext. M-l 
at the foot is not stated. It will appear on perusal of Ext. 
W-l that this order is also a photo copy and its original copy 
with tho workman has not been filed. It is quite possible 
that the lower portion of the order in which the reason for 
suspension and the note of receipt of the copy under the 
signature of the concerned workman has been omitted in 
Ext. W-l while obtaining the photo copy of the ortFr of 
suspension dated 23-3-82/ It appears therefore that although 
the details of misappropriation of fertilizer and shonw 
of empty guny bags is not stated in the suapens f on Order 
the management had given an indication as to why the con¬ 
cerned workman wa« being put on suspension. Regulation 
66 of the FCI Staff Regulation, 1971 deals with suspension. 
Regulation 66(1)(A) shows that the disciplinary authority 




{Urn-II —*nr j(ii)] ttwt g, 19 9 a/ tiffm u, 1912 4417 


may place an employee under suspension where a disciplinary 
piocniuin# ti^di'iisJ iiiiii is contemplated or is pending, Thm 
the Stalf Regulation which admiLicdiy gives the service condi¬ 
tion of its employee has clearly laid that an employee may 
be placed under suspension even when a disciplinary proceed¬ 
ing against him is contemplated. Thus it was not necessary 
fOT the management to give the details of the allegation for 
which the concerned workman was being suspended. How* 
ever, in the present case the management has indicated the 
reasons for the suspension of the concerned workman. The 
management therefore was within their rights under the FCI 
SudF Regulation to suspend the concerned workman as a 
disciplinary proceeding against him was under contempla¬ 
tion. 

Admittedly the concerned workman filed Title Suit No, 
42^88 in which although he did not succeed before the 
Muns ; ff, he succeeded before 'he District Judge in obtan- 
jtig order of injunction staying the operation of the order of

sion order dated 23-3-R2 was revoked by the management

It appears from the case of the management that when 
they were informed of the institution of the case by the CBI 
avamst t^r, concerned workman mder prevention of corruption 
Act and Tnd : an Penal Code the management again placed

Poipt No. 2 

In view of my finding oni point No. 1, I further hold that 
the action of the management in dcny’ng full wages and 
other benefits to the concerned workman even after the 
revolution of the two | suspension orders is noi legal 
and justified. It has been submitted on behalf of the 
workmen that even his pay has not been revised by the 
management with effect from l-S-83 although all the staff 
of FO have drawn their arrears but the pay scale of the 
concerned workman has notj been re\i$ed. The management 
stated that as the matter 1 regarding his suspension and 
punishment in the CBI case is pending consideration, the 
salary of the concerned workman has not been revised under 
rne revised pay scale of thk PCI. There is no ground for 
not revising the pay scale of the workman as lie was put on 
suspension. The management was bound to fix his pay in the 
revised scale from the date *T came into force in respect ot 
the other workman of FCI. The management has shown no 
rule or order to show that p$y of g n employee of FCt cannot 
be revised during the period of his suspension. I hoM there¬ 
fore that the management must revise the pay scale of the 
concerned workman from tfie date other workman of the 
FCI have been allowed revised pay scales. 

I hold that the action of the management in denying 
revised full wages and other benefits to the concerned work¬ 
man even after revokation of the suspension order and 
resumption of his duties is not legal and justified.

2. The instant case wks taken up for cross examination 
of the workman on 10-5-90, when the workman did not 
turn up. On the said date it was ordered that a notice be 
sent to the workman fixing 28-6-90. On 28-6-90 the work¬ 
man appeared but the cross examination of the workman 
could not over on account of the fact that the notice was 
sent to the Union which raided the depute and not to 
the workman whereas it ought to have bexn seal to 
workman as he him c elf appearing in the case. As c u*h one 
nice nrpnrtimitv was given to him for his cross examination 
and 27-8-90 was fixed n the case. From the oidc sheet 
it appears that he put hi?) initials on the order sheet in token 
of having come to know about the next date, in the 
case. On 27-8-90 he d'd not turn up despite having full 
knowledge about the date and purpose of the case. 

3. Thus from the above, it appears that neither the Union 
nor the workman himself is interested hi prosecuting the 
case otherwise he should have presented before the Tribunal 
for his cross examination. As such a no claim award is 
given : n the case against the workman/Union. 

4. Reference is answered accordingly.

For the management—Shri N. K. Zakhmi. 

INDUSTRY : Banking. STATE : Punjab. 

AWARD 

Cental Govt, vide Gazette notification No. L-12012]325| 
86-D. 11(A) dated 3rd December 1987 issued U/S 10(l)(d) 
of Industrial Disputes Act 1947 referred the following dis¬ 
pute to this Tribunal for decision on a dispute raised by 
Shri Kasturi Lai : 

u Whether the action of the management of State Bank 
of Patiala, is justified in declaring the services of 
Shri Kasturi Lai Cashier-cum-Clerk as abandoned 
because he availed long unauthorised leave? If not, 
to what relief the workman concerned is entitled?” 

2. Present case was taken up at Lok Adalat today. The 
parties have amicably settled the dispute. Shri P. P. Tandon 
Industrial Relation Officer' appearing on behalf of the 
management has stated that Kasturi Lai has offered re¬ 
employment from the date of his joining latest by 1st 
August 1990. His salary will be fixed by giving four incre¬ 
ments earned by him during the previous employment from 
1972 to 1976 and in addition he will also be given four 
more increments without any arrears in this regard. He 
has also stated that workman has to forgo his claim for 
back wages and other monetary benefits in this regard and 
also forgo any claim of seniority and continuity of service 
and ether service benefits except seniority for the period he 
had already served. 

Kasturi Lai workman has accepted this offer of re-employ¬ 
ment offered by the management on the terms and condi¬ 
tions stated above. 

3. In view of this statement that parties have settled the 
dispute and the workman has approved the offer made by 
the management thus: a no dispute award is returned. 
Chandigarh.

in giving seniority to Shri Vishnu Datt over Shri 
Ram Kishan SHM, Printing Press, Shakurbasti is

SHM entitled?” 

2. Brief facts of the case are that Sh. Ram Kishan work¬ 
man was an employee of the Printing Prcss^24orthcrn Railway 
Shakur Basti and was initially appointed as Khalasi in Gr.

Roller-Caster in Gr. 75—110 and ex-cadre post by passing 
all the rules in connection with the posting & Promotion in 
this connection and was promoted as Roller-caster in Gr. 
110—180 on 16-5-64, Mr. Dass was again promoted as 
Mechanic (Ex cadre) in Gr. 130—212 on 1-12-66. Shri 
Ram Kishan applicant who was eligible candidate for the 
promotion of Mechanic Gr. 130^-212 being a fitter protested

130-212 wr.o hrid oa 10-9 6 j and Visnnu i^'utL and Asiiok 
iuiigii AoIuWtiiia y** 4s allowed to appear in ine LUKte test 
uioni.wdfi oner iLair or Pics^ ignoring all die provisions,

Vishnu LXitt Eutjr from jrgadnan Unit pi evented hiniGelt 
in the Rahway i lini ng iTcss, Ciliaturbasu lo-il-6? and

Thereafter ditleienee orders were passed directing

Dutt could not be pul renior lo him and it was a gross

Since the appointment to the Printing Pre^s Shakur B^sti

5. On these grounds after con.iuenng tho matter the 
reference was made for adjudication to this court. 

6. The railway management in its written statement ellcg* 
cd that Shi i Vishnu Dull was transferred to Railway Print¬ 
ing Press after reversion on his own request in the scale 
of 110—180 and not in the scale 130—212 and tho fixation 
of seniority was made according to the rules and the work¬ 
man was not entidod to any relief as claimed by him. 

7. T have heard the Id. representative for the parties and 
have gone through the record pmdured. Die workman him¬ 
self nnpeared as WWl while the Management p^odu^ed 
G. L, Batra MW1 to support their case. The main dKmRc 
between the paries before me as pointed out by their res¬

was in the interest of Administration or at his request. It 
the appointment of Vishnu Dull was at his own request then 
ho could not be made senior to the senior most person work¬ 
ing there. It is not disputed that both the Units i.e. Shakur- 
basti Printing Press and Jagdish Workshop were two diffe.ont

own statement. He has also referred to Ex. M-5 vide skilled 
Vishnu Dutt highly skilled fitter was reverted as skilled 
fitter at his own icqucst and transferred under Supe: iniendent 
Printing and Stationery, Northern Railway Shakur Basil. 
He has further referred to Rule 312 of I.R.E.M. Chapter Ill

‘‘Rule 312.—Transfer on request—the seniority of Rly. 
Servant transferred at there own request from one 
Rly, to another should be allotted below that of 
the existing confirmed & offering Railway seivama 
in the relevant grade in the promotion ground in 
the new establishment irespective of the date of 
confirmation or length of offering service of the 
transfered Railway servant." 

8. The representative for the Management on the other 
side has referred to an order dated 20-11-67 sh No. 12 ‘B* 
in which it is stated that the transfer of Vishnu. Dutt is in 
the interest of Administration and not at his request and 
his seniority may be fixed accordingly. Whenever any trans¬ 
fer is made in the interest of Administration the seniority 
rule 311 would bo applicable and the seniority of Vishnu 
Dutt in his case has been regulated by rule 311 of I.R.E.M, 
Chapter III. 

9, On hearing the representative for the parties the only 

point for determination whicn emerged before me was as 
to whether his transfer was at his request or in the interest 
of Administration. The Management was directed to pro¬ 
duce the original letter vide which his transfer was effected. 
That was never produced and even a perusal of the W.S. 
filed by the Management does not show that the transfer 
was in the Administrative Interest. It simply alleges that 
he was transferred in the Administrative interest and his 
seniority was correctly assigned according to rule 311 of the 
Establishment Manual. The Management evfcn duiing the 
stage of arguments was directed to produce the order and 
produce the original application of Vishnu Dutt but the 
Management, failed to produce the same. From the docu¬ 
ments and the evidence produced it appears .that aome case 
was pending against Vishnu Dutt in his own workshop of 
Jagadhri where some action was being taken against him 
and h e opted for demotion from the post of highly rolled 
fitter. Though the original request of Sh. Vishnu Dutt has 
not been brought on record which cannot be believed to 

have been mbpla ed, I am of the view that Sh. Vishnu 

Dutt was transferred from the Jagadhri Workshop to the 
Prm+ing Press at his own request and not in the interest of 
Administration, The notice Ex, Ml5 dearly shows that 

f'-om hiohlv "killed fitter he was reverted as skilled fitter at 
hiv own ^equ^st and transferred under Printing & Stationery, 
Northern Railway, Shakur Basti, There was no question of 
anv Administrative ground taken as ground for transfer 
and T, therefore, hold that the seniority given to him a''cord- 
tw to nde 311 of the Manual was not ju c tified and his 
-prdoniv wa« rrnu'red to he fixed under rule 312 of the 
r eV ant nfie" ref or ed above T. therefore, order ihat the 

c emo^+v of Ram Kishnn and Vi"hmi Dutt be fixed acco^dinv 
to rule 312 of the Rahway Establishment Manual, This 
r*f„ rnr)re disposed of accordingly.

AWARD 

The Central Government by their Order No. L-12012/10/ 
89-D.IUA) dated 8th August, 1989 have referred the follow¬ 
ing industrial dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication undei 
Section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947:— 

"Whether the action of the management of Dena Bank 
in dismissing from service Shri B. V. Kalambakar 
is justified, If not, to what relief is the workman 
entitled?” 

2. The Senior Manager, Personnel filed his written state¬ 
ment (Ex. 2) in support of the action in question taken by 
the Bank management. 

3, The workman was duly served with the notice of the 
present reference. He had appeared before the Tribunal in 
person on ,19th September, 1989 and his representative ap¬ 
peared on 28th March, 1990. The reference was received 
by this Tribunal on 14th August, 1989 and since then the 
matter was adjourned from time to time to enable the work¬ 
man or his Union to file their statement of claim. However, 
no statement of claim was filed by either of them. Therefore, 
the management was directed to prove its case. Accordingly 
the Chief Manager (Personnel), Shri Govind R. Nevrckar 
filed his affidavit (Ex. 4) in support of the action taken by 
the Rank management against the workman Shri B. V. 
Kelembckar, As the workman and his rcpiesentativc remain¬ 
ed absent, what is stated by the 'Chief Manager (Personnel), 
Dena Bank in support of the act‘on taken by the Bank has 
gone unchallenged. Iff substance the Chief Manager (Per¬ 
sonnel) stated thus:— 

“That while the workman Shri R. V. Kelembekar was 
working in the subordinate cadre at our Bombay 
City Regional Office he stole draft No, 468689 
dated 7th January, 19S5 for Rs, 1997 favouring 
Dena Bank and issued by Canara Bank, Bhawani 
being the proceeds of DBC No. 2508 dated 22nd 
December, 1984 for Rs. 2000 Account M's. Natio¬ 
nal Dye Chem sent in collection by our Ballard 
Estate branch. Since our Ballard Estate Branch 




4423 


[flW It-Cfr 3 (ii)] IRTCr ■FT fl/ f I 990/ *TTtt*T 14. 1912 


did not receive the draft in question, on enquiry 
it came to light that the workman had deposited 
the said draft in his 5.R. account No. 5021 at the 
Tardeo branch of Rajapur Tuluka, Janata Sahakar* 
Bank Ltd. The amount of the draft was, howevci, 
not credited to his account as the misconduct came 
to light by them. 

The workman again caused theft of pay slip No. 
1239880 dated 28th December, 1984 for Rs. 222 
favouring M/s. National Automobiles Pvt. Ltd. and 
prepared by cmr Bombay City Regional Office. He 
again tried to deposit the same in his SB account 
No, 27843 with the 13ank T s Main Office on 1st 
February, 1985 which however was detected and 
not credited, He thereafter a’tempted to deposit the 
same in his SB n/c No 3287 with the Bank's Prin¬ 
cess Street branch on 21st February, 1985 by tam¬ 
per irtg/putting up wrong endorsements when again 
the same was detected and the matter was reported 
to our City Regional Office 

The workman had also on 20th February, 1985 
tampered with Bank's muster roll and on the same 
day at about 12.30 p.in. entered the cabin of Asstt. 
Regional Manager and ihc Regional Manager and 
abused them shouting “Sbil Asher Ko Ak«l Nahin. 1 

“Ho was therefore issued with charge sheet dated 9th

levelled against him:

(b) Committing fiaud on the Bank; 

(c) Tampering with Bank's record; 

(d ) Wilful insubordination ot disobedience of any 
lawful and reasonable order ot the Management 
or of a superior; and 

(e) Doing any act prejudicial to the interest of the 
Bank, involving or likely to involve the Bank in 
financial loss.” 

“At the departmental enquiry the workman was given

fairly in compliance with the principles of natural 
justice. On conclusion of the same the Euquiry 
Officer held the workman guilty of all the charges

of ‘Dismissal without notice from the services ot 
the Bank 1 on the workman vide order dated 21st 
August, 

‘‘That the workman being aggrieved preferred depart¬ 
mental appeal when again the Appellate Authority 
finding no reason to interfere with the decision of 
the Disciplinary Authority confirmed the punish¬ 
ment of ‘Dismissal’ imposed on the workman by 
the Disciplinary Authority.” 

4. I find that the misconduct committed by the said work¬ 
man is quite serious and he deserved the punishment of dis¬ 
missal from service of the Bank. Therefore, the action taken 
by the Bank management is quite just and proper. 

5, The following Award is therefore passed. 


award 

The action of the management of Dena Bank in dismissing 
from service Shrl B. V. Kelambekar is justified. 

The parties to bear their owit costs of this Reference.

AWARD 

The Central Government in the Ministry of Lacom vide 
its Order No, L-12012/109/84-D.ITA dated 6-12-84 has re¬ 
ferred the following industrial dispute to this Tribunal tor 
adjudication: 

“Whether the action of the management of Allahabad 
Bank, in terminating the services of Shri Rani Babu 
Sharma, sub-staff from October* 1979 while; return^ 
ing his juniors in the Service and not considering 
him for further employment when subsequently re¬ 
cruiting fresh hands, is justified? if not, to what 
relief is the workman concerned entitled?” 

2, Thq brief facts of this case are that Shri Ram Babu 
Sharma, th e workman' concerned initially joined Allahabad 
Bank as a temporary peon in June 1976 at Delhi Gmcu 
Ghaziabad Branch, He wag further given tempo¬ 
rary employment at the same branch and at two other 
branches at Ghaziabad from, time to time. No employment 
was given to the workman after October, 1979. According 
to the workman he had worked for 303 days dm ing the 
year 1976 to 1979 though tho actual days of work mg "comes 
to 293 days. The services of the workman were lerminated 
in October, 1979 while officials junior to him were retained. 
Since the workman had completed more than 240 days so 
he was entitled to regular employment as was done in the 
case of other employees who had completed the same period, 

3. The management on the other hand alleged that there 
was inordinate delay in moving the matter on behalf of the 
workman. However, the main point urged by the manage¬ 
ment was that the workman find not worked for 240 days 
during any 12 calendar months. Since this requirement of 
law was not fulfilled so he was not entitled to any regular 
employment. 



4424 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 6, 1990/ASVINA 14, 1912 


[Part II —Sec. 3(ii)] 


4. The workman appeared himself as WW1 and Shri 
Rajindcr Prakash Garg MW1 on behalf of the Management. 
J have heard representative for the parties and have gone 
through the record, During the course of arguments when con¬ 
fronted with the record the representative for the workman 
conceded that the workman as per record has not been able 
to prove that he worked for 240 days continuously during 
the period of any 12 calendar months He has further con¬ 
ceded that since it is requirement of law the workman was 
not entitled to be given regular employment. However, the 
representative for the management accepted the demand of 
the workman representative that in case any further recruit¬ 
ment of peons is made this workman shall also be called for 
test/interview if any and shall be considered for this post : s 
and when it occurs. 1, therefore, while holding that the 
termination of the services of the workman was justified by 
ihc management direct that in wase of any further recruit¬ 
ment in this category cf employees, the workman shall be 
considered by the management and shall be called for inter¬ 
view/tost if any as per rules. Parlies are left to bem their 
own costs of this dispute.

By Older No. L-200l2/126/88-D.lII(A)/D-lV(A), dated, 
the 31st October, 1988, the Central Government in the Minis¬ 
try ol Labour has, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
cluiC'-e (di of sub-section (1) of sub-section (2-A) of the 
Industrial Deputes Act, 1947, referred the following dispute 
lor adjudication to this Tribunal: 

’Witcl her ihc demand of the union for Technical Grade 
TT for Subrata Das, Bhatta Supervisor, Laikdih Deep 
Colliery, Chanch-Victoria Area No. XU of M/s. 
B.C.C, Ltd. with effect from 13-2-1979 is justified ? 
If so, to what relief the concerned workman is 
entitled T’ 

2, The ci.se of the sponsoring union, namely, Husitlriya 
Colliery Mazdeor Sangh, as disclosed in the written state¬ 
ment submitted on bealf of the workman is that Subrata Das, 
the concerned workman was originally posted at Bhagaband 
Colliery in 1971. He was transferred to Laikdih Deep Col¬ 
liery on 13-J0-80. lie was getting the salary equivalent to 
Technical Grade T)’ as Bhatta Supervisor in 1974. He has 
been praying for higher grade from time to time, but that 
was not conceded to. With his tiansfer to Laikdih Deep 
Colliery his work load became much higher but he was not 
granted any increase in his remuneration nor was he given 
any incentive. He, however, continued praying for higher 
grade and pHy scale with retrospective effect. Sometime in 
1986 when the management of Laikdih Deep Colliery found 
that he would not relent nor would he give up his claim 
for Technical Grade l B', it obtained the grade and pay 
structuie of Hard Coke employees from another colliery of 
M/s. B.C.C. Ltd., namely. Sendra Bansjora Colliery which 
corroborated that Bhatta Supervisors are in Technical Grade 
*B\ The grade and pay structure as clarified by the Sendra 
Bansjora Colliery is followed in other collieries too For 
example, 5/Shri Surajdeo Singh and S. P. Singh, Bhatta 
Supervisor of Damoda Colliery and Kooridih Colliery of 
M/s, B.C.C. Ltd. respectively arc also in Technical Grade 
k B\ but the workmen concerned has been kept in Technical 
Grade TV. Anyway, even if clarification and recommendation 
of the colliery management for allowing the workman con¬ 
cerned Technical Grade *B\ the Area management did not 
concede to it, IL iR alleged that this is a case of flagrant 
discrimination because when the Bhatta Supervisors in other 
BG.C.L.’s collieries have been given Technical Grade *B\ 
the workman concerned, working as Bhatta Supervisor is not 
getting Technical Grade ’fl 1 though he has got the same 
qualifications as the other Bhatta Supervisors have, The union 
tried to settle the issue with the management, but no settle¬ 
ment was possible due to unrealistic and adamant attitude 
of the management. In the circumstances, the union has 
prayed that its demand for Technical Grade TT to the work¬ 
men concerned with retrospective effect from 1974 is justified, 

3. The case of the management of Laldih Deep Colliery, 
as disclosed in the written statement submitted by the man¬ 
agement, details apart, is as follows : 

The present reference is not maintainable. The concerned 
workman was transferred to Laikdih Deep Colliery of Chanch- 
Victoria Area (No. XID in October, 1980 as Bhatta Super¬ 
visor and was fixed in Technical and Supervisory Grade ‘D’ 
taking inta consideration his the then existing condition of 
service at the time of his transfer from Bhagaband Colliery* 
Tho Coke Bhatta of Laikdih Deep Colliery was closed for 
many years and reopened in December, 1985. During the 
above period the concerned workman was put on alternative 
job because of closure of Coke Bhatta, As soon as Coke 
Bhaitu was reopened in December, 1985 he was posted back 
as Bhatta Supervisor to look after the Hard Coke Oven. 
His case was considered for promotion in 1986 and he was 
promoted from Technical and Supeivisory Grade TV to Grade 
‘C’ 1 with effect fiom 22-5-1986. As he had not yet com¬ 
pleted minimum three years experience in Grade *C\ his 
case for promotion from Qradc *C 5 to Grade ‘B* Could not 
be considered. Supervisory personnels are placed in non- 
Techtvcal Clerical Grade 111 to Special Grade or Technical 
Grade TV to Technical Grade L A* depending upon the nature 
and quality of supervision, extent of control exercise by a 
supervisor over the workmen and extent of responsibility 
shouldered by him. There exists cadre scheme for different 



4425 


[sfFr 3 (ii)] 35T WRT : 6, 1990,/mfoPT 14, 1912 


trades, but there exists no cadre scheme for Bhatta Supervi¬ 
sor and they are promoted accoidhig :o individual merits. 
Bhatta Supervisors remain hi clerical grade so long as he 
supervises the workmen employed in the Coke Oven Plant 
and entile technical job is supervised and controlled by Assit. 
Manager or Foreman-in-charge. After he acquires skill and 
technical knowledge, he is considered for his conversion to 
Technical and Supervisory Grade *D’ or ‘C* as the case may 
be. In the course of years he is promoted from Grade ‘D 
to Grade ‘C’ and from Grade ‘C 5 to Grade on the 
basis of Ins merit. Thus, a Bhatta Supervisor may be placed 
in any grade from Grade TV to 1 Grade 4 B’ or from Grade 
III to Grade X. The concerned workman has been given 
proper grade appropriate to his merit, and he cannot claim 
Grade ’B , on the basis that his designation is Bhatta Super¬ 
vise . 

4. In rejoinder to the wrdten statement of the management, 
the sponsoring union has stated that the cocerned workman 
has been representing for his proper placement in fixation 
of his grade and scale of pay in Grade *B\ since he was 
earlier employed at Bhagaband Colliery as Bhatta Supervisor. 
Although the Coke Bhatta of Laikdih Deep Colliery was clos* 
ed for a short while, he was saddled with alternative job 
with no less responsibility than the job of Bhatta Supervisor. 
His case is for placement in proper grade and not a case for 
promotion. 

5. In rejoinder to the written statement of the sponsoring 
union, the management has reiterated the facts as disclosed 
in the written statement and asserted that he was not in 
Technical and Supervisory Grade ‘D’ in 1974. 

6 . The sponsoring union has examined the concerned work¬ 
man and laid in evidence two items of documents which 
have been marked Exts. W-l and W-2. 

On the other hand, the management has examined MW-1 
A. K. Mazumdar, posted as General Manager of Area No, 
XII of M/s. B.C.C Ltd. from February, 1984 to May, 1988 
and laid in evidence a number of documents which have 
been marked Exts M-l to M-5. 

7. The sponsoring union has di>ciosed in the written state¬ 
ment that the concerned workman, Subrata Das, was origi 
nally appointed at Bhagaband Colliery in 1971. But there 
is no vestige of evidence to prove this statement of fact. 
Even the concerned workman figuring as WW-1 has not 
stated that he joined service of Bhagaband colliery 
in 1971. On the other hand, he has 
stated that in 1974 he was appointed as Bhatta Supervisor 
and posted to Jhurkunda Coke Plant under Chanch Victoria 
Area. Admittedly, he was transferred from Bhagaband colliery 
to laikdih Deep Colliery and that he joined Laikdih Deep 
Colliery on 13-10-80. Office Order relating to his porting 
dated 13-10-80 (Ext. M-2) and his joining repo r t submitted 
on the same date (Ext M-3) bear out this position. The last 
pay certificate isued by the Manager. Bhagaband Colliery 
dated 3-11-80 (Ext. M-4) indicate* that his date of appoint¬ 
ment was 7-2-74. Thus, the evidence on record establishes 
the fact that the concerned workman joined the sendee of 
M/s, B.C.C. Ltd. on 7-2-1974. 

8 . The next contention of the concerned workman is that 
he ha* been working as Bhatta Supervisor since 1974 and 
was placed in Technical Grade TV in 1974. 

9. The last pay certificate of the concerned workman 
issued hv the Manager of Bhagaband Colliery dated 3-11-80 
(Ext. M-4) indicates that he was on Technical Grade TV 
a* the time of Irs transfer from Bhagaband to laikdih Deep 
Colliery. 

Shri B. JnsJv, learned Advocate for the management, has 
contended that the date of appointment of the concerned 
workman is mentioned in L.P.C. but hi* status as a workman 
has not been disclosed as Bkatla Supervisory m 1974, 
In fact there can be no e«cane from the position that he was 
working as Bhatta «nre r vi*or in 1974. The concerned work¬ 
man has emphatically stated in his testimony that in 1974 
he was appointed as Bhatta Supervisor and was posted to 
Thurkunda Co^e Plant under Chanch Victor!* Area and that 
he was placed on Technical Grade TV. He has not been 


cross-examined on this point. Besides, the noting sheet of 
the Senior Personnel Officer of Laikdih Deep Colliery indi¬ 
cates that the concerned workman was in Technical Grade 
*D* since 1974. This position has been reiterated by the 
Dy. P.M. in the noting sheet (Ext. W-2). The oral evidence 
of the concerned workman together with the documentary 
evidence mentioned above (Ex. W-2) leads me to conclude 
that the concerned workman was working as Bhatta Super¬ 
visor and placed in Technical Grade TV with effect fiom 
sometime in 1974. 

10. The case of the sponsoring union is ihat since Bhatta 
Supervisor in other collieries of M/s. B.C.C. Ltd., namely, 
Sendra Bansjora Colliery, Bamoda Colliery and Kooridih 
colliery are placed in Technical Grade ‘S’ and since the 
concerned workman being a Bhatta Supervisor employed in 
Laikdih Deep colliery of M/s. B.C.C. Ltd. in similar capacity, 
he is entitled to be placed in Technical and Supervisory Grade 
C B'. By way of illustration, the union has stated S/Shri 
Surajdeo Singh and S. P. Singh, both Bhatta Supervisors, 
Damoda Colliery and Kooridih Colliery respectively are 
placed in Technical Grade *B\ In addition, the union has 
asserted that Bhatta Supervisors are placed in Technical 
Grade B 5 in Sendra Bansjora colliery. The concerned work¬ 
man has stated, in support of his claim, that Coke Oven 
Supervisors are entitled to get Technical Grade *B\ for ins¬ 
tance, Coking Oven supervisors in Sendra Bansjora Colliery, 
Koondih Collieiy and Damoda Colliery are getting Technical 
Grade TV. Besides, the sponsoring union has produced a 
photo cony of the letter dated 21-2-86 written by the Agent 
of Sendra Bansiora Colliery to the Supdt, Laikdih Deep 
Colliery stating that Hard Coke Bhatta Supervisors are placed 
in Grade <T V m Sendra Bansjora (Ext. W-l). The union has 
also relied neon the noting sheet initiated by the Senior 
Personnel Officer of the concerned workman for his upgra- 
datfon in Technical Grade ‘B’ as Bhatta Supervisor (Ext. 
W-2). 

The case of the management is that there exists no cadre 
scheme for Bhatta Supervisor and they are promoted accord¬ 
ing to individual merits and that Supervisory personnels are 
placed in non-technical clerical Grade-TF to Special Grace 
or in Technical Grade ‘D’ to ‘A’ depending upon the nature 
and quality of supervis’on and extent of control exercised 
by a Supervisor over a workman and extent of responsibility 
shouldered by him. Bhatta Supervisors mav be placed from 
Grade TV to ‘B* or from Grade-TTI to Grade-T and the con¬ 
cerned workman ha* been properlv promoted from Technical 
and Supervisory Grade TV to Grade £ C J with effect from 
22-5-1986. The management has produced the promotion 
policy of Lovnbod Coke Plant as disclosed bv the Sundt., 
loyabad Coke Plant by its letter dated 30-10-89 (Ext. M-5). 
Tn terms of this disclosure it appear* that Oven Supervisors 
are placed in Technical Grade *C’ and there is no Bhatta 
Supe**v ; sor in I oyabad Coke Plant. 

11. Admittedly, M/s, B.C.C. Ltd. has got cadre scheme 
for workmen of different disciplines/trade*., but it has got 
no cadre scheme for Bhatta Supervisors. As a matter of fact 
the designation of Bhatta Supervisor does not find place in 
the nomenclature, iob description and categorisation of emp¬ 
loyees working in the Coal Industry. 

The concerned workman has admitted in his evidence that 
Bhatta is the Hindi eouivalent of coke over and that there 
exists coke o^ens of different types, such as, Bee-hive, Bye- 
products Coke Oven and Country tyre oven. He has admit¬ 
ted that he has got no technical qualification although he 
has got techn : cal experience. From bis past service career 
it appears that h* has got technical experience as Bhatta 
or Coke 0\ep ^imervisor. The concerned workman was placed 
in Technical G r nde TV wdule working in Bhagaband colliery 
as Bhatta Supendsoi. It appears that he joined his nost at 
Laikdffi Deep Colliery on transfer as Bhatta Supervisor in 
T^chnkH Crude TV without demur. Thus, from the evidence 
on record It is established that Bhatta Supervisors are placed 
in Technical Grade TV to Grade ‘B’ by the management. 

It is disclosed from the evidence of the concerned workman 
that there was different types of C‘>kc ewens—Bee-hwe. Bve- 
nroduct and Conntn tvpe coke ovens. There is no evidence 
on record to md^te similar nature of supervision by 
the Bhatta Supervisor is required for all these types of coke

ovens and the workmen working there. There is also no 
evidence on record with regard to the educational and tech¬ 
nical qualification of Bhatta Supervisor in Sendra Bansjom 
Colliery, IJamoda Colliery and ivooridih Collieiy. Theie is 
also no evidence on record that the concerned workman 
has got similar qualification both educational and technical 
as the Bhattii Supervisors of the aforesaid collieries have got. 
No evidence has been laid to show that the coke ovens ot 
the aforesaid collieries are of similar in nature as the coke 
oven of Laifcdifi Deep Colliery. This being the state of 
evidence, it appears that a large area of hard material facts 
essential for consideration of comparable situation or cir¬ 
cumstances has remained grey. So, the demand of the spom 
soring un>n for placement/upgradation of the concerned 
workman is not based on solid facts and evidence, 

12. Sfiri M. K. Sengupta, learned Advocate for the spon¬ 
soring union and the concerned workman, has submitted that 
the lower echelons of the management have strongly recom¬ 
mended the case of the concerned workman for his place*

(Ext, W-2). Indeed* in the noting sheet Sr. Personnel Officer

and Supervisory Grade ‘B* and it appears that the Dy, P.M. 
also dittoed such placement or upgradation. But the General

and promoted him to Technical and Supervisory Grade f C’ 
hohling that promotion from Grade ‘D’ to Grade *B' is not 
ghi'u Lfntil and unless extraordinary performance is done. 
The General Manager agreed to his promotion from Grade 
TP to Grade l C’ and accordingly, as per written statement 
of the management, the concerned workman was promoted 
from Grade ‘D’ to Grade C f with effect from 22-5-1986 

Admittedly, General Manager of the Area is the controlling 
authority. Anyway, while the Sr, P.O. and Dy. P.M. recom* 
mended the case of the concerned workman for his upgrada* 
tiem in Grade *B\ the General Manager considered the case 
as a case of promotion But it appears that the General 
Manager was very specific in his order; h£ has remarked 
that he does not agree with the recommendations of Dy. 
P.M, and Supdf. (M), However, the fact remains that the 
concerned workman has been designated as Bhatta Supervisor 
since the date of his joinmg sometime in 1974 and has ac¬ 
quired considerable experience ‘as Bhatta Supervisor although 
he has got no technical qualification. Shrl B. Josh! has sub¬ 
mitted that the coke oven of Laikdih Deep Colliery remained 
closed from 1980 till December, 1985 and the concerned 
woikman was put on alternative job. There is no dispute 
that the concerned workman was working on alternative iob 
while the coke oven of Taikdlh Deep colbery remained closed, 
But although he did not work during that ^criod as Bhatta 
Supervisor Tii'- claim for further promotion to Technical 
Grade ‘B’ cannot be ignored because he was put on the 
alternative iob not for the fault of his, but presumably due 
to some managerial constraint. The plea of the- management 
discloses that the case of promotion is considered after com¬ 
pletion of tffiee veers experience in a particular post. The 
concerned workman was promoted from Grade ‘D* to Grade 
with effect from 72-5-86 and so his care for promotion 
is ripe for consideration bv the management after exp ; ry of 
three years from the date of his promotion. Tn the circums¬ 
tances of the case. T direct the management to consider the 
case of promotion of the concerned workman from Techmcal 
Gmde T’ fn Grad* ‘R' immed^atclv. preferably within two 
months from the date of publication of the award.

Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the 

Central Government hereby publishes the award of 
the Central Government Industrial Tribunal No. 1, 
Dhanbad as shown in the Annexure in the industrial 
dispute between the employers in relation to the 
management of Sudamdih Colliery of M|s, Bharat 
Coking Coal Limited and their workmen, which was 
received by the Central Government on 3-9-1990.

Kamgar Union, as disclosed in the written statement 
submitted on behalf of the concerned workmen,

since its inception. They have been working con¬

put in 240 days attendance, Haradhan Mahato and 
14 other concerned workmen whose names and other 
particulars have been disclosed in Anncxurc TV ot 
the written statement had been working as perma¬ 
nent slurry cleaning mazdoors and M|.. B.CC Ltd. 
has provided them employment. The remaining con¬

Annexes ‘A* and TT but appear in the order of

cleaning mazdoors, but they were stopped from duty 
after the present industrial dispute was raided. Ail 
fhe concerned workmen have been working as sluiry 
'Cleaning mazdoors within the precinct and premi cs 
of Sudamdih Washery for long. The job of slurry 
cleaning is a permanent nature of job and all the 
implements required for execution of the job are 
being supplied by the management. The concerned 
workman hav been performing permanent nature 
of job under the direct control and supervision of 
the management, but even then, the management 
has been disbursing their wages through different 
intermediaries posing contractor. The manage¬

workmen throiigh a Co-operative which has got no 
control and supervision over the workmen. The o- 
called Co-operative has no right or power to engage 
any fresh hand. It is alleged that disbursement of

nut a legal camouflage. The workmen of Giddi ‘A’ 
of M|s. Central Coalfields Ltd, arc getting Group-Ill

3. The management of Sudamdih Washery has 
contested the claim of the sponsoring union preferred 
on behalf of theconcemed workmen. The case of 
the management, as disclo ed in its written statement 
cum rejoinder bereft of details, is a: follows : 

.The present reference is bad in law and not main¬ 
tainable since there exists no relationship of employer 
and employee between the management of the one 
hand and the concerned persons on the other. The 
reference v also in violation of the provisions of 
Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act and 
based on representation on facts. The substantive 
case of the management is that Sudamdih Washery is 
one of the coal vvasheries of M|s, Bharat Coking Coal 
Ltd. which is a Government company within ihe 
meaning of Section 617 of the Company’s Act and 
wholly financed by the Central Government. The coal 
washery in quest on, like other coal washeries, has 
been constructed for the beneficiation of coal (impro¬ 
ving the quality of coal by removing ash, moisture

etc.) and to make it suitable for use in th<? steel plants 
tor steel smutting. The process of washingjbeneficm- 
nun of coal ,n washery is such that some hue particles 
ol coal How oui ytengwith the special liquid solution 
used for the paipose of washing coal* This ts called 
sluiry which t s collected in the ponds situate outside 
the wushery premises and the water which is consti¬ 
tuent of slurry is removed aud fine particles of coal 
are salvaged and stacked. When these fine particles 
fit w out of the washcry with slurry assume below a 
given size and quality, the slurry is called taling or 
rejects. Since fine particles of coal and their qualities 
above a specified s ze and quality represent washed 
et ai which can be used in steel plant, the management 
of washeries make constant effort and endeavour by 
inTallat-on of necessary cuipments and modernising 
the equipment and taking various other methods 
having a bearing on the relevant technology to reduce 
:ut flowing ,ou + of such fine particles of coal as a part 
of slurry and to eliminate the same as far as possible. 
^Vhcn the ash content and other impurities in the 
composition of slurry exceed a specified norm, the 
siurry assumes the form of ta ; lings]rejccts and it js 
no longer necessary to collect it, Sudamdih Ccal 
Washery was constructed in 1980 through a Contrac¬ 
tor on a tum-key contract basis. At the t ; me of cons¬ 
truction of wafft-ry some workers were employed by 
the contractor. VvTen the construction of washery was 
temple ted there was no work for the contractor or 
lus men. At mat tune, Shri A. K. Roy, was the M. P. 
of Dhanbad having been elected in 1979 general elec- 
I'on who was also the Prcs : dcnt of the sponsoring 
union i.e. B lur Colliery Kaingar Union and is still

Nation before Dy. Comm : ssioner, Dhanbad, deman¬

vvoikcrs of the contractor of Sudamidh Washery 
(‘reject on the gtound that they were local Adivasis. 
The workers were retrenched by the contractor wlio 
was engaged in job of construction and fabrication ol 
die Washery Project. The then Dy. Commissioner, 
Duanbad, riU'uveiifd in the matter and at his instance 
!?Ud on the deiiund of Shri A. K. Roy, President, 
B.C.K. Union, the management of M)s. B.C.C. Ltd. 
agreed to provide job of slurry cleaning|remova! work 
at Sudamaih Wc^hery to some of the workers of the 
ex-contractor subject to the workers concerned for¬ 
ming a Co-operaUve Society. The formafion cf labour 
co-operative was also commanded by National Com- 
rairs on of Labour appointed by the Central Govern¬ 
ment (1969). In pursuance of the said arrangement 
the cx-workers of the ex-contractor formed a Co¬ 
operative SocHy which was registered under B : hat 
Co-operative Society Act, 1935 and it was named as 
Kamgai ShramiV Sahyog Samity, The Co-operative 
Src'ety obtained n licence under Contract Labour 
(Regulation & Abolition), Act, 1970 and a regular 
contract agreement had been executed between the 
management and the Co-operative Society concerned 
and the contract was rencwed[extended from time to 
lime till now. The B,C,K. Union raised the purported 
dispute before the AT.C (C), Dhanbad in connection 
with thi matter in September, 1986 and admitted 
that the persons concerned were the workers of the Co¬ 
operative Society and that an Advisory Board set up 
under Section 10 of the Contract Labour (Regulation 
& Abolition) Act had not recommended that the con¬ 
tract system in the job of slurry cleaning|remova1 
should be prohib ted. The dispute raised by B.C.K 
Un’on relates to abolition of contract system in slurry 


removal work in Sudamdih Washery and departmen¬ 
talisation ol the vvuikmen and absorption ot the wor¬ 
kers ot the Co-operuve Society which has been per¬ 
form ng me work,executing the contract. The demand 
Ivi abouUon of contract system in any purtxtfiar job 
or establishment cannot constitute the subject matter 
el an industrial dispute and such claim has to be 
dealt with under Section 10 ot the Contract Laoout 
(Regulation & Abolit on) Act as per decision of the 
Hon bic Supreme Court and other High Courts, The 
Contract Labour Central Advisory Board set up under 
Section 10 of the Contract Labour (Regulation & 
Aboliton) Act by the Central Government constituted 
a Sub-Committee in February, 1977 for considering 
a* to whether the job relat ng to recovery of slurry in 
coal washcry should be prohibited from contract 
system under Section 10 of the said Act, The said 
Committee recommended by its report submitted in 
1978 that the job relating to removal of slurry should 
not be considered for departmentaiisatin for the time 
being. The Central Government has accepted the re¬ 
commendation, Subsequently in 1986 the aforesaid 
Committee constituted another Sub-Committee for the 
same purpose and according to the information of Hi© 
management the sa d Sub-Committee has since sub¬ 
mitted its report and it is under the consideration of 
the Board. Therefore, the question of abolition ol 
contract system in slurry removal work would depend 
on the decision ol the Board. In the meantime the 
Kamgar Shramik Sahyog Samity has inflated a number 
of workers purported to be working in slurry removal 
woik although there is absolutely no justification foi 
such a huge number. The management has carried out 
Industrial Engineering Studies and has come to the 
conclusion that the: present quantum of slurry removal 
work does not rcqu'rc even 200 workers. The spon¬ 
soring union apart from the present dispute involving 
253 persons, raised another dispute before A.L.Ch(C),

the management should regularise another 298 per¬ 
sons on the gromid that they are working m some 
sluiry removal work. The action of the sponsoring 
union indicates that it has resorted to deliberate 
MODUS OPERAND] of inducting additional persons 
w'th a view to ultimately passing on the burden to the 
management in the public Sector. The Co-operative 
Society inducted additional persons without justifica¬ 
tion from year to year. The office bearers of the sporn 
-oring union and the persons who managed the affairs 
of the Co-opcrpth/e Society are more less the same 
persons. Anyway, the workers employed by the Co¬ 
operative Society arc the workmen of the Society and 
there is absolutely no employer and employee relation¬ 
ship between the management and these workmen. The 
persons employed by the Co-operative Society are all 
the share holder cf the Society. Technical expert and 
specialist have been going into the question of redu¬ 
cing the quantum of slurry. The slurry removal work 
in the present from is neither permanent nor does it 
require any szeabb number of employees. Considering 
all these facts and circumstances the union is estopped 
from making the demand which cannot be considered 
as industrial dispute for adjudication. It is pertinent 
to mention that the sponsoring union took up the 
question of provision of employment to ex-workers 
of erstwhile contractor and put their number as 106 
and demanded employment for only 30 of them. Out 
of these 30 workmen, the management provided em¬ 
ployment to 28 persons and the matter stood closed,

4136 THE GAZE7TE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 6, 1990/ASVINA 14, 1912 [Pari 11—Sbc, 3(ii)J 


total elimination cf flow of slurry as suggested by

is yet to make its presence felt. In the context ot

come to the concision that the job of slurry|reclama-

In view of the hard evidence on all fronts, 1 am cons¬ 
trained to come- to the conclusion that the workmen 
working in the slurry pond are really the employees 
of the management of Sudamdib Washery of MK 
B.C.C. Ltd. and hence there exists relationship of 
employer and employee between the management of 
Sudamdih Washery and the concerned workmen 
working in the slurry pond. I am fortified in *his 
position by the decision reported in 1978 Lab. T.C. 
1264 (Hussainbhai Vs, Alath Factory TezhiLili Union 
and others). 

Shri R. S. Murtliy, learned Advocate for the manage¬ 
ment has submitted that the Hon’ble Supreme Court 
has departed from the law as propounded in the deci¬ 
sion cited above by the later decision reported in 
(i) 1985 (II) LLJ.4 (Workmen of Food Corporation 
of India Vs. MU. Food Corporation of Tndia Ltd.) 
and (ii) 1987 Lab. LC. 32 (Krishna Kurtip Vs-. Gene¬ 
ral Manager, Gujrat Refinery). But upon a perusal 
of these decisions 1 am constrained to hold that the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court has neither departed from 
its earlier position as reported in 1978 Lab. LC. 1264 
nor the decision reported therein has been diluted 
in subsequent decision of the same Hon’ble Court. 

13. Then again Shri Murthy has urged before mr 
that the present reference is not maintainable became 
the sponsoring union has demanded for abolition of 
contract system. Shri D. Mukherje#* authorised 
representative of the workmen, has submitted that 
no such demand has been made by the sponsoring 
union. It will not appear from the terms of refe¬ 
rence as weli as frdm the pleading of the sponsoring 
union that the demand has not been made for aboli¬ 
tion of contract system in reclamation of slurry from 
slurry pond. It does not appear from the evidence 
that the entire body of workmen working in the slurry 
pond has demanded for their regularisation. Hence, 
there is no reason to hold either in facts or in law 
that the sponsoring union has demanded for aboli¬ 
tion of Contract labour System in reclamation of slurry' 
slurry removal ^Votk. 

14, The. present reference is whether the demand 
of the sponsoring union for regularisation of 253 
workmen listed in the annexures is justified or not- 
It appears from the pleading of the sponsoring union 
that at present out of the 253 workmen only 118 are 
working in. tire slurry pond as disclosed in Annexure 
‘A’ to the written statement. Annexure TT to the 
written statement discloses that 15 other workmen 
have been regularised in service and the remaining 
workmen have been stopped from dutv by the man¬ 
agement. Since none of the workmen stopped from 
duty have come forward to assert their claim for 
■ rcgularisation, T have no hesitation to dismiss their 
claim. I will onlv consider now, the claim of 118 
vverkmen as Frted in Annexure ‘A’ to the Pleading 
of the sponsoring union for their rceularisaffon. It 
appears from the testimony of WW4 Ballav Chandra 
Das that Satruthnn Mondal listed in Annexure tf A* to 
the pleadinr of the sponsoring union has been given 


employment by the m^nagemMit So, only 117 work 
men nre now left fot my consider alien. 

15. In view of the fact that 117 workmen as des¬ 
cribed in the Annexure ‘A 1 to the pleading of the 
sponsoring union, have been working in the slurp' 
pond tor reclamation of slurry and they are, in 
fact, the employees of the management and not the 
employees of the Co-operative vSociety which is con¬ 
sidered as camouflage, these workmen are entitled to 
be regularised in service with effect from the date of 
referencee i,e, 25-5-1989 and back wages. The man¬ 
agement of Sudamdih Washery is really the principal 
employer of these workmen in view of the evidence 
on record. Legally also these workmen are the work¬ 
man of the management of Sudamdih Washery of 
Mis. B.C.C. Ltd. The management has produced the 
licence of the Co-operative Society marked Ext. M4). 
But it has failed to produce registration of its estab¬ 
lishment. The provision of Contract Labour (Regu¬ 
lation & Abolition) Act envisages that for valid emp¬ 
loyment of contract labour two conditions must be 
fulfilled, namely, (i) every principal employer of an 
establishment must be registered and (ii) the contrac¬ 
tor must have a valid licences. The mere holding of 
licence by the contractor alone will not enable the 
management to treat the workmen as contract labour. 
When these conditions arc not satisfied tile workmen 
involved should be considered as the workmen 
of the principal employer legally. (Current Labour 
Reports- —Vol, 3—-March, 1990—page 363—United 

labour Union & others Vs. Union of India 
^ Ors.), Thus, (lie management of Sudamdih 
Wa herv is the principal and real emplover 
these ! 17 workmen as listed in Annexure ‘A’ to the 
pleading of the sponsoring union. 

Shri Ghose (MW-1) has admitted that the con¬ 
cerned workmen have been stacking slurry on the 
bank of the pond after excavation. " Normally then- 
job is two-fold—lifting of slurry from the slurry pond 
and stacking the same on the. bank of the pond. The 
management has fix<*d the minimum work load of 
every workmen as 3.1 cubic metres. This being the 
position, the Concerned workmen arc entitled to get 
Grirnp III wages as available to Stacker in N.C.W.A.I. 
which is followed up in subsequent N.C.W.As, 

16. Accordingly, the following award is rendered 
fU ' demand of Bihar Colliery Kamgar Union, Dhan- 
hank of the pond after cxcavaiton. Normally their 
Sati^ughan Mondal as' disclosed in Annexure ‘A’ to 
the pleading of the sponsoring union for reeularisa- 
tion in service of Mis. Bharat Coking Coal Ltd, is 
justified. The management is diretced to regularise 
them in service' with effect from the date of reference 
he. 25-5-1989 and pay them back wages as per 
Group-Ill minus the amount already paid to them. 

Tn the chcumstances o f the case, I award no cost.

The present reference arises /out o~ Order No,

By Order .No. L-24U12(.252),87-D.1V(,B), dated, 
the 13th June, 19SE, the central Government in the 
Ministry of Labour lias, in exercise of the powers 
conferred by clause (d> ot sub-section (1) of Section 
10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, referred the 
following dispute for adjudication to tms Tribunal ;■ 

“Whether the action ot the management of Gidi 
Washery ot ’central Coalfields Ltd. in not 
absorbing {departmentalising S|Shri Dhan- 
eshwar-1, (2) Duria Swami, (,3) Budhan-I,
