tite Settlement dated 9-4-82, is justified *> 
If not, to what relief the workmen concern¬ 
ed are entitled ?” 

2. The case of the management of Gidi Washery 
of Central Coalfields Ltd., as disclosed in the writ¬ 
ten statement-cum-rcjoinder of the employer, details 
apart, is as follows ; 

The present reference is not maintainable inas¬ 
much as the order of reference is based on errone¬ 
ous presumption. The substantive case of the mana¬ 
gement is that Gidi Washery is one of the units of 
Argada Area of Central Coalfields Ltd. where bene- 
ficiation of medium coking coal is undertaken for the 
purpose of supply to the Steel Plain. The washery 
has an elaborately tqnipped plant and machinery for 
the purpose of washing of medium coking coal. One 
of the jobs which was occasionally carried out in the 
washery was plant cleaning. This job was awarded 
to a contractor. The recognised union of the mana¬ 
gement i.e. Rashtriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh affi¬ 
liated to Indian National Trade Union Congress made 
a demand on the management that the mazdoors en¬ 
gaged in plant cleaning in Gidi Washery should be 
employed departmental^ by the management on the 
ground that plant cleaning is a permanent and con¬ 
tinuous job. The matter was negotiated between the 
management and the recognised union and it was 
agreed that such of the plant cleaning mazdoors of 
Gidi Washery employed by the contractor MT. Sen¬ 
gupta & Sons and who were on the roll of ‘Gidi Was¬ 
hery on 1-1-52 and who had worked continuously 


during tne calendar year 1-1-81 to 31-12-81 and 
who nad pui in 240 uays of attendance during tout 
puma would oe caret uy. employed by tile manage - 
iiitQi of Uidi Wasnery as Category-! mazdoor. AC- 
coidmgiys a settlement was leactied) ociween the 
management and Kasnlriya colliery Mazdoor Sangh 
on ^-4-82. Tne management implemented the settle¬ 
ment and all tne workers ot mis. Sengupta <k Co. 
engaged as plant cleaning mazdoors in Gidi Washery 
were provided employment oy the management. The 
rcasniriya Colliery Mzdoor Sangh accepted trie posi¬ 
tion mat there was no other prant Cleaning mazdoor 
previously engaged by Mj». Sengupta & Sons kit out 
tor employment. 'The register ot tfte contractor show¬ 
ing tne names of workers who were engaged by Mis. 
Sengupta & Sons as plant cleaning mazdoor was 
closed on 0 - 8 - 82 . It transpired later that as a result 
of some manipulation and collusion between interes¬ 
ted parties and the persons concerned some more 
names were subsequently inserted after 6-8-82. This 
naturally led to suspicion and it was revealed that 
the persons whose names were added were either 
deployed as TruckjCar Driver or as domestic ser¬ 
vant or in some other capacity by tbe contractor for 
his personal service and that an attempt was made by 
manipulation of records to show that some additio¬ 
nal persons had worked as plant cleaning mazdoors. 
When the aforesaid manipulation and fraud were 
detected by the management, steps were taken to see 
that the employment was not pro\ided to the persons 
whose names were added by manipulation and fraud 
after 6-8-82. Since the persons concerned were not 
covered by Tripartite Settlement reached between the 
ji, .n. : ;.-iiK and Rashtriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh 
■; ii. ■ Officer on 9-4-82 and since the 
persons concerned were not mployed as plant clean¬ 
ing mazdoor at any time, the question of the mana¬ 
gement absorbingjdepartmentalising them did not and 
could not arise. The sponsoring union, namely. Bihar 
Colliery Kamgar Union, has made this demand as a 
result of manipulation and as a result of collusion 
between the interested parties and fraud committed 
by such interested persons. Hence, he question of 
giving relief to the concerned persons does not arise. 
I 11 the circumstances, the management has submitted 
tiiat its action in not absorbing!regularising the con¬ 
cerned persons be held to be justified.

The concerned workmen had been working in Gidi

Appreciating the legal position the management en¬

plant cleaning mazdoors. Th£ local management is 
very much biased and prejudiced against the mem- 
Cjl, of Bihar Colliery Kaxngar Union and is alwcp 
on the look out to victimise the members of this 
union* When the management came to know that the 
concerned workmen \wre the members and suppor¬

allowed to resume their duties in spite of interview, 
appointment letters and medical examination. All) 
other plant cleaning mazdoors have been absorved| 
departmentalised in service. The concerned workmen 
and their union represented the matter before the 
management against the illegal and discriminatory atti¬ 
tude, but to no effect. Seeing no other alternative, 
the union raised an industrial dispute before the 
A.L.C'XC), D'hanbad, which ended in failure due to 
the adamant attitude of the management. The appro¬ 
priate Government, however, was please to refer the 
dispute for adjudication by this Tribunal. The action 
of the management of Gidi Washery in not absorb- 
ingjdcpartmentalisin hgt econcemed workman is ille¬ 
gal and unjustified and qgainst the principles of natu¬ 
ral justice. In the circumstances, the union has pray¬ 
ed that the concerned vorkmeu be absorbed or de¬ 
partmentalised in service with retrospccti^^cct and 
paid arrears of wages and consequential benefits.

fied.

our Officer, inserted the names of the concerned

in the pleading not lifts the management submitted

nation this witness has admitted that Sri Srivastava

from service, he will not be released until and unless

that Die resignation letter of Shri Srivastava and ac¬ 
ceptance of his resignation must be lying with the

gedly played dirty trick with 'it. There is no answer 
to this question. 

I 11 the circumstances, I have reason to beelieve that 
the management has made Sri Srivastava a scape¬ 
goat in order to lend credence to its case that the

ned worlrtnen. Then again, it remains unexplained as

days. The evidence of these two witnesses firmly 
establishes the position that the concerned workmen 
had worked in Gidi Washery as plant cleaning maz¬ 
doors continuously and that in 1981 too they had 
worked for more than 240 days. 

The sponsoring union called for from the mana¬ 
gement Bonus Register by petition dated 23-1-1989. 
The management produced the Bonus Register (Ext. 
W-4[1) purported to be that of Mis. Sengupta & 
Sons. But the first entry in he regiser (Ext W-4) 
indicates the name of the contractor as M[s. Malgu- 
ram. The other pages of this register do not disclose 
the nam,;, of the contractor. Hence, this register 
cannot be accepted' as Bonus Register of M|s. Sen- 
gunta & Sons. Shri D. Mukherjee has criticised 
th»s register bv submitting that the management 
attempted to subvert the real fart bv producing this 
curious register 1 . AnVWav. the fact is that the real 
Bonus Register of Mis. Senguota <& Sons has not 
been Produced. The management has not taken the 
plea that such register is not in its custody or it was 
not made available to them by M!s. r Sengunta & 
Sons. This being the position I come to the con¬ 
clusion that the renl fact would have come to light 
had the Bonus Register of M’s. Sengupta & Sons 
been produced. 

11. However, fiom the evidence on record. T come 
to the ernch^ion that the concerned workmen wdfc 
emnlnvcd under Mk Senigimfa & Sons as plant 
demine mazdoors of OrI t Washerv and that in 
1981 all of them bad nut in more than 240 days 
of attendance Hence, the concerned workmen are 
entitled to be absOrbedldcpartmentalised in service

fWT<! J flij] 

ol the Washery. Now, the question remains from 
which period they are entitled to be so absorbed or 
departmentalised. 

12. According to MW~1 M.M. v i ur.au the subse- 
oiunt settlement mari ;d Ext, M-2 was effected in 
August, 1982; It is or, the basis ol this settlement 
that some o» the plant cleaning mazdoox of M;s. 

Sengupta & Sons were absorbed [departmentalised in

were issued in November. 1982. Toss fact is borne out

i:. icached that these plain cleaning mazdoors got

cvrned workmen were \ repared in J983 and MW-2 
Sri Sin ha has testified that out of these seven con¬

to three persons. These letters of appointment

concerned workmen, has stated that he worked in

for the same period. This being so, the concerned 
workmen are cntided to be absorbed [departmentalised 
in the service of the washery from 18-2-1983 as 
Categpry-I Mazdoor and to get back wages from that 
date till they arc allowed to join their duties by the 
management. Nevertheless, in the circumstances of 
the present case I consider that the management 
should pay them 50 per cent of back wages. 

13. Accordingly, the following award is render¬ 
ed-—the action of the management of Gidi Washery 
of Central Coalfieds Ltd. in not a^sorbingjdepart- 
nicntalising SfShri (i) Dhrmeshwar-E (2) Duria 
Swami, (3) Budhan-I, (4) Ratneshwmy (5) Sibu, 
(6) Dhaneshwar-II and (7) Gurdayal Singh as per 
tripartite Settlement dated 9-4-1982 and Subsequent 
settlement (Ext^M-2) is not justified. The manage¬ 
ment is directed to absortf[departmentalise the con¬ 
cerned workmen as Categolry-1 Mazdocrs with effect 
from 18-2-1983 and to pay them 50 per cent of back 
wages from that dale till the date of their joining 
duties. The concerned workmen are directed to 
report for duties within one month from the date of 
publication of this award. 

In the circumstances of the case, T award no cost.

12. Regard being had to the discrepancy in tno

Hence, the report of me Enquiry Officer holding the 
concerned workman guilty o i the charge and tin? 
action of the management in dismissing him from 
service cm the basis of the report of the Enquiry Officer 
are not sustainable. Accordingly, the concerned werk- 
man Is entitled to be reinstated in service, but in the 
circumstances of the case with 50% of back wages. 


Presiding Officer.

It has been submitted by the management that the 
departmental enquiry was fair and proper and was 
conducted after observing fully the principles of natu¬ 
ral justice. It is furtner submitted that it may first be 
heard whether the departmental enquiry was fair and

the management may be given a chance to establish the

In view of the facts that the domestic enquiry was 
being challenged by the concerned workman the ques¬ 
tion whether the domestic enquiry was fair and pro¬ 
per was heard first as a preliminary issue in the case. 
The management examined witness in die preliminary 
enquiry and the concerned workman also examined 
himself. By the order dated 28-10-88 the Tribunal 
held that the domestic enquiry held into the charges 
against the concerned workman was not fair proper 
and in accordance with the principles of natural jus¬ 
tice and the management was given opportunity to 
adduce evidence afresh before this Tribunal. 

Now the point for decision is whether the dismissal 
of the concerned workman from service with effect 
from 17-8-85 was justified. 

The management examined MW-3 Kasturi Lai 
Shanna, MW-4 Shri A. M. Khan and MW-5 Shri

have been made in respect of which the concemed

workman has been dismissed from service. The said

,L>. bJngu, Head aurveyor iouiiu mat an unuumoriseu

charged under clause 19(5) of the Standing Orders lor 
the baid misconduct. The standing order of the com¬ 
pany is r,xi, ivi-y. Clause 19(5) or the Standing orders 
snows that tor drunkness, lighting, riotous or disorderly 
or indecent behaviour an employee may bo suspenaeu, 
fined or dismissed. the concerned workman has 
ocen chaiged under this clause for his indecent man¬ 
ner. The facts relating to the alleged indecent man¬ 
ner is disclosed in the chargeshect itself and will 
show that on being asked to demolish the unauthorised 
construction, the concerned workman refused to do 
so and started shouting in a very indecent manner. 
The indecent manner appears to be in the utterances 
of the concerned workman and the specific words

mas of the chargesheet. The words “JO KARNA HO 
KARIYA, MAIN GHAR BANAUNGA. ROKNA 
HO TO ROKIYE, AUR ANJAM DEKH1YF 1 does 
not actually disclosed as to what is the indecent man¬ 
ner in the said utterances of the concerned work¬ 
man. However, we may look to the evidence ad¬ 
duced on behalf of the management as to what ac¬ 
tually constituted “Indecent manner* for which the 
concerned workman was charged and was dismissed 
from service. MW-4 i s Shri A. M. Khan, Security 
Inspector who had found the concerned workman 
making construction over the encroached land of the 
management. He had stated that on 4-6-85 at about 
11-00 A.M. he had gone for inspection along with 
Shri D. Singh, Head Surveyor at llie residence of the 
concerned workman. He has stated that he saw the 
concerned workman making unauthorised construc¬ 
tion over company^ land afid a *ked the concerned 
workman not to make construction over Company’s 
land but the concerned wrokman did not agree and 
became angry saying do whatever you have to do and 
you will feel the consequences. Thus it will appear 
from his evidence that the concerned workman refu¬ 
sed to remove the alleged construction from the com¬ 
pany’s land and became angry saying him to do what¬ 
ever he liked for which he will feel consequence. The 
utterances may amount to disobedience of what Shri 
Khan ordered but yet it is a question whether the said 
disobedience will amount to misconduct under the 
Standing Oders of the management. In cross-exami¬ 
nation MW-4 has stated that it was a part of hi iob 
for the infection of the company's land and that he 
had not with him anv paper regarding: the title of the 
company's land on which the allegation of encroach' 
ment is being made against the concerned workman. 

It will also appear from his evidence that MW-4 had 
not gone earlier for inspection of thot place and as 
such it was not possible for him to say whether the 
alleged encroachment by construction 5va$ being made

tor the first time. He has stated that the concerned 
vvoik'ijruui was constructing room on the company's 
jaud and that adjacent to it is the land or the con¬

be tore. Thus it is admitted that the concerned work¬

Security Inspector Shri A. M. Khan docs not actually 
disclose the indecent manner of the concerned work¬ 
man except that he had refused to obey his order for 
removing the alleged construction and had told him 
to do whatever he liked to do in the matter, 

MW-5 is the Head Surveyor who had gone along 
•with MW-4. He ha^ stated that fie along with ann 
Khan nad gone tor inspection ot Bhelatand Area m 
June, J985 and saw the concerned workman making 
construction over the land of the company. He has 
stated that the said land was demarcated by pillar to 
snow that it was company’s land. He has stated 
mat they asked the concerned workman not to make 
construction over company’s land. The concerned 
workman did not pay any heed to the words and in¬ 
sisted that he would make construction over the said 
land and told them to do whatever they liked to do 
and then they will feel the consequences. MW-5 has

his office to inspect the house of the concerned work¬ 
man for which he had not been instructed by any 
higher authority to inspect the same and it was his 
routine job. He has further stated that he was not 
carrying the survey map along with him as it was not 
required for inspection and he ha s not filled the sur¬ 
vey map in this case. Ho has stated that the con¬ 
cerned workman was disputing that the construction 
was on his own land. It will thus appear from his 
evidence that the concerned workman was claiming 
that the land belonged to him over which the cons¬ 
truction was being made, Jt will further appear 
from his evidence that he had not mentioned of the 
survey map in his depositions before the enquiry offi¬ 
cer. He had stated that the pillars demarcating in the 
company’s land was' fixed in the year 1965-66 but 
he was not present when the pillars were fixed by 
the management. The evidence of MW-5 therefore 
shows fh&t the concerned workman was claiming the 
land as his own in respect of which the allegations 
are being made by the management. Neither MW-4 
Shri Khan nor MW-5 the Head Surveyor prepared 
any map or any report to show the extent of the en¬ 
croachment alleged to have been made by the con¬ 
cerned workman over the company’s land. The evi¬ 
dence of MW-5 further does not disclose any. inde¬ 
cent manner of the concerned workman in courses 
of his dutv or ot the establishment where the concer¬ 
ned workman performs his duties.

chargesheet which is marked Ext, W-2 in the case 
and is the same as Ext. M-l -filed by the management.

Ext, W-i is the reply of the concerned workman 
dated 18-0-85 in respect of the managements icice-- 
dated /ine management nu s not hied tn>’ 
saul leUtT dated V|iU-o-di oy wmch the concilium 
workman was instructed tor demolishing uttauluoMseu

woiKman stated m £,xt. W-l tnat there was no new 
construction and that only due to the reason that a 
puiiion oi n.s nou-.e tail down mat he was re-construe- 
niig Uic same, lie also stated that the claim oi the 
management be clarified in the light of registration 
papeis and with the help of Amin and that if any 
construction attached to his house falls in the com¬ 
pany’s land he was ready to demolish it* The con¬ 
cerned workman further demanded from the man¬ 
agement to arrange to remove the overhead line pas¬ 
sing over Ills house as he is intending to construct 
the upper storey on his present construction. The fact 
that the overhead electric line is passing over the 
said house of the concerned workman is an admitted 
fact. MW-4 Shri M. Khan had stated that 11 K.V, 
electric transmission line wire were passing over the 
bouse of the concerned workman since long. Ho has 
further stated that passing of 11 K.V. transmission 
line over his house is dangerous. He stated that he 
cannot say it the concerned workman had objected at 
the time when the transmission line was installed on 
the ground that the house of the concerned workman 
was standing there since long and that the transmis¬ 
sion line should not be taken over his house. From 
Ext, W-l it will appear that the concerned workman 
had demanded from the management for the remo¬ 
val of the overhead line passing over his house and 
thi s must have annoyed the management as the 
management will have to incur expenditure over the 
sarnie. It was suggested to MW-4 that the concer¬ 
ned workman had intact filed a case for removing 
the transmission line and thereafter the management 
had removed the said overhead line passing over 
house of the concerned workman, MW-4 had no 
doubt expressed ignorance of the case and its refill) 
but the management had no courage to examine any 
of its witness to falsify that the concerned workman 
had filed a case for removing the transmission line 
possing over his house and that the same was re¬ 
moved thereafter. 

It has been held in 1983 Lab I. C, page 1909 that 
numerous acts of misconduct such as drunkenness, 
fighting, indecent or disorderly behaviour, use of abu¬ 
sive language, wrongfully interfering work of other 
employees are not perse misconduct and each of them 
have co-relation to the time and place where it is 
committed. Such acts of misconduct would be mis¬ 
conduct punishable only if committed within the pre¬ 
mises of the establishment or in the vicinity thereof. 
If a workman is involved in a riot indulging in field¬ 
ing somewhere far away from the premises of the 
establishment It has no casual connection with ncr- 
formane'e of his duty in the industrial mt 

in wfdch he is cmnlovccT. Tt was aLo held in the 
said fLdrion that to enaKle an rmnlovcr to t.ence- 
fiillv onrrv on >iiq indiKtrm] activity th^ management 

have ™4th ‘■rtoUv'/v tn nrr scribe condi¬ 

tion of service mrlndinfr enumerated acts nf -miscon¬ 
duct mil ah cotnnilflcd uidiin the nrerm/.''s of the 

The r-muloyi-j/r,. ha/s hardM am oxtua' 
territorial iurisdicrion. He is not the custodian of 


general law and order situation nor the viuiu or 
mcjiior of his workmen for their well regulated cub 
Turin advancement, ii the power to regumte the be- 
ha\Jour oi tne workmen outbade the duty hours and 
.a any pu.ee wherever tney may be was conferred 
upon the employer, the contract of service may b- 
reduced to contract oi slavery. The employer is en¬ 
titled d) prescribe conditons of service more or less 
specifying the acts of misconduct to be enforced vvhh- 
in the premises where the workmen gather together 
for rendering service. The employer lias both power 
and jurisdiction to regulate the behaviour ol the 
workmen within the premises* of the establishment 
or for peacefully carrying the industrial activities in 
the vicinity of the establishment. Having the above 
principle in view it wilt appear that the concerned 
workman was claiming his right title and possession 
over the land alleged to have been encroached by 
him and which is being claimed as the land belonging 
to the management. There is absolutely no authentic 
evidence to show that the land alleged to have been 
encroached and belonging to the management was in 
taet the land of the management. The fa t that the 
concerned workman had at the very outset while re¬ 
plying tc\ihe allegation made by the management had 
stated in Ext.-l that the land over which he wag cons¬ 
tructing belonged to him since long and if the man¬ 
agement had any doubt he could get the land measure 
by the Amin. The management neither got the dis¬ 
puted portion of the land measured by anv Amin nor 
the surveyor of their company came to a definite con¬ 
clusion that any portion of the alleged land over 
which the concerned workman was making construc¬ 
tion Dl within, the area of the land of the manage¬ 
ment. I have already stated above that there is 
tto evidence to show that the alleged encroached land 
actually belonged to the management of Tisco and as 
such it was too much for the management to ask the 
concerned workman to remove the construction being 
claimed by the concerned workman. The dispute 
over the land was out and out a Civil dispute reemir- 
ing a decision from the competent court so that the 
rich* title and possession over the disputed land coutd 
be decided. 

If the land being claimed by the management ac¬ 
tually belonged to the concerned workman and he had 
his construction on it fince long, it cannot be said that 
the concerned workman had disobeyed any lawful 
order of the management's officer and hence the con¬ 
cerned workman was within bis right to refuse to re¬ 
move the encroachment unless it was measured and 
found that there was any encroachment by the: con¬ 
cerned workman over, the land of the management. If 
in the above background the . concerned workman told 
the Security Inspector to do anything he liked to do 
and for which he would feel the consequences, there 
was nothing to establish that it was an indecent man¬ 
ner constituting misconduct in course of duties of the 
concerned workman. The house of the concerned 
workman is away from the establishment where, the- 
concerned Workman performs his duties and the place 
where the facts happened was awav tom the estab¬ 
lishment where the concerned workman performs b?s 
duries. As such it was b’evond the jurisdiction of the 
marmi?;t'inent to rone in the concerned workman for 
indecent-behaviour outside his duty hours and pifee 
of duty. All these facts lead us to a conclusion that

the. charge of indecent- manner constituting misron 
duct under clause 19f5) of the Standing Orders jvas 
not been established by the management and as such 
the di-missal of the concerned workman is also not 
justified. 

In (lie result, I hold that the action of the manage¬ 
ment of >iji:a Colliery of Mjs. Tisco in dismissing the 
coiici’- 1 . d -erbium Sliri Ram Prasad Suo, Timber 
Mistry from service with effect from 17-8-85 is not 
justified. The management is therefore directed to 
reinstate the concerned workman with effect fioin 
17-8-85 and to pay him all the back wage.-: and con¬ 
sequential benefits from the date of his dismissal 
within 2 months from the date of publication of the 
Award. 

This is mv Award.

New Delhi, the 19th September, 1990 

S.O. 2675.—Whceras a vacancy has occurred in the 
Office of the Presiding Officer of the Labour Court 
with headquarters at Jaipur constituted by the noti¬ 
fication of the Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour S.O. No, 1780 dated the 19th June, 1963 ; 

Now, therefore in pursuance of the provisions of 
Section 8 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
uf 1947) the Central Government hereby appoints 
Shri Babulal Goyal as the P/esiding Officer of the 
Labour Court constituted as aforesaid.

New Deffi', the 20th September, 1990 

S.O, 2676.—Whereas the Central Government is 
satisfied that the public interest requires that the 
irvicc in the Bank Note Press, Dewas (MP) which 
is covered by entry 22 in the First Schedule to the In¬ 
dustrial disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), should be 
declared to be a public utility service for the purpose 
of the said Act; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of tbe 
Induvtr al Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), he Cent¬ 
ral Government hereby declares with immediate effect 
the sa ! d industry to be a public utility service for the 
puiposes of he said Act for a period of six months.
